NOTICE
NOTICE is hereby given that the Fifty-second (52nd) Annual General Meeting of the Shareholders (Members) of MMP 
Industries Limited will be held on Monday, the 8 th day of September 2025 at 11.00 A.M. through Video Conferencing 
(‘VC’)/Other Audio Visual Means (‘OA VM’) facility, to transact the following business: -
ORDINARY BUSINESS
1. To receive, consider and adopt the Audited Financial Statements (Standalone & Consolidated) of the Company for 
the year 2024-25 ended 31st March, 2025, comprising of the Balance Sheet as at 31 st March 2025, Statement of 
Profit & Loss and Statement of Cash Flow for the year 2024-25 ended 31st March, 2025, together with the Report 
of the Statutory Auditors and Board’s Report thereon.
2. To declare a final dividend of Rs. 2/- per equity share for the year ended March 31, 2025.
3. To appoint a Director in place of Mr. T. N. Murthy, (DIN -08342116), [Category –Executive], who retires by 
rotation and, being eligible, offers himself for re-appointment.
SPECIAL BUSINESS
4. Ratification of Remuneration of Cost Auditors of the Company
 “RESOLVED THAT pursuant to the provisions of Section 148 and all other applicable provisions, if any, of the 
Companies Act, 2013 read with the Companies (Audit and Auditors) Rules, 2014 (including any statutory modification/s 
or re-enactment/s thereof, for the time being in force), M/s Deepak Khanuja & Associates, Cost Accountants, Nagpur 
[Firm Registration No. 100247], whose appointment as the Cost Auditors of the Company, for the financial year 2024-
25 ending 31 st March 2025, has been duly approved by the Board of Directors based on the recommendations of the 
Audit Committee of the Company, be paid a sum Rs.70,000/- (Rupees Seventy Thousand) only plus applicable tax 
(GST) and reimbursement of actual out of pocket expenses, if any, as a remuneration for audit of cost records of the 
Company for the financial year 2025-26 ending 31 st March 2026, as recommended by the Board of Directors based on 
the recommendations of the Audit Committee of the Company, be and are hereby ratified.”
5. Appointment of Mr. Sachin Nirgudkar as a director and as an Independent Director
 To consider and if thought fit, to pass with or without modification(s), the following resolution as a Special 
Resolution:
 “RESOLVED THAT Mr. Sachin Nirgudkar (DIN - 06890618) who was appointed by the Board of Directors 
as an Additional Director(Independent, Non-Executive) of the Company with effect from 8 th August, 2025 and 
who holds office up to the date of this Annual General Meeting of the Company in terms of Section 161(1) of the 
Companies Act, 2013 (“Act”) and Articles of Association of the Company but who is eligible for appointment, 
in respect of whom the Company has received a notice in writing from a member under Section 160(1) of the 
Act proposing his candidature for the office of Director of the Company, be and is hereby appointed as Director 
(Independent, Non-Executive) of the Company.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT pursuant to the provisions of Sections 149, 152 and other applicable provisions, 
if any, of the Act, the Companies (Appointment and Qualifications of Directors) Rules, 2014, read with Schedule 
IV to the Act and Regulation 17 and other applicable regulations of the Securities and Exchange Board of 
India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (“SEBI Listing Regulations”), as 
amended from time to time, the appointment of Mr. Sachin Nirgudkar who meets the criteria for independence 
as provided in Section 149(6) of the Act along with the rules framed thereunder and Regulation 16(1)(b) of SEBI 
Listing Regulations and who has submitted a declaration to that effect, and who is eligible for appointment as an 
Independent Director of the Company, be and is hereby appointed as an Independent Director of the Company, 
not liable to retire by rotation, for a term of five years commencing 8 st August, 2025 to 7th August 2030 be and is 
hereby approved.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT Board of Directors of the Company be and are hereby severally authorized to 
do and perform all such acts, deeds, matters and things, as may be considered necessary, desirable or expedient to 
give effect to this resolution.”

NOTES:-
1. The General Circular Nos. 14/2020 dated April 08, 2020, No. 17/2020 dated April 13, 2020, No. 20/2020 dated May 
05, 2020 and various subsequent Circulars latest being No. 09/2024 dated September 19, 2024 issued by the Ministry 
of Corporate Affairs (collectively referred to as “MCA Circulars”) permitted the holding of the Meeting through Video 
Conferencing/Other Audio Visual Means (VC/OA VM), without the physical presence of the Shareholders at a common 
venue. In compliance with the provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 (the “Act”), SEBI (Listing Obligations and 
Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (“Listing Regulations”) and the MCA Circulars, the Annual General 
Meeting (“AGM”) of the Company is being held through VC/OA VM. The venue of AGM shall be deemed to be the 
Registered Office of the Company.
2. As the AGM shall be conducted through VC / OA VM, physical attendance of members is not required and the facility 
for appointment of Proxy by the Members is not available for this AGM and hence the Proxy Form and Attendance Slip 
including Route Map are not annexed to this Notice.
3. The Explanatory Statement pursuant to Section 102 of the Act setting out material facts concerning the business under 
Item Nos. 4 to 6 of the Notice is annexed hereto. The relevant details pursuant to Regulation 36(3) of the Listing 
Regulations and Secretarial Standards on General Meetings (SS-2) issued by the Institute of Company Secretaries 
of India (ICSI), in respect of Directors seeking appointment/re- appointment at this AGM are also annexed.  
Mr. Arun Raghuvirraj Bhandari and Ms. Rohini Bhandari are relatives of each other as defined under Section 2(77) of 
the Companies Act, 2013.
4. The Members can join the AGM in the VC/OA VM mode 15 minutes before & after the scheduled time of the 
commencement of the Meeting by following the procedure mentioned in the Notice. The facility of participation at the 
AGM through VC/OA VM will be made available to at least 1000 members on first come first served basis. This will not 
include large Shareholders (Shareholders holding 2% or more shareholding), Promoters, Institutional Investors, Directors, 
Key Managerial Personnel, the Chairpersons of the Audit Committee, Nomination and Remuneration Committee and 
Stakeholders Relationship Committee, Auditors etc. who are allowed to attend the AGM without restriction on account 
of first come first served basis. The detailed instructions for joining the Meeting through VC/OA VM forms part of this 
notes.

5. Institutional / Corporate shareholders (i.e. other than individuals, HUF, NRI, etc.) are required to send a scanned copy 
(PDF / JPG Format) of their respective Board or governing body Resolution / Authorisation etc., authorising their 
representative to attend the AGM through VC / OA VM on their behalf and to vote through remote e-V oting. The said 
Resolution / Authorisation shall be sent to the Scrutiniser by e-mail on its registered e-mail address to cshelpdesk09@
gmail.com, csvaibhavj@gmail.com with a copy marked to companysecretary@mmpil.com.
6. The attendance of the Members attending the AGM through VC/OA VM will be counted for the purpose of reckoning the 
quorum under Section 103 of the Act.
7. In line with aforementioned MCA and SEBI Circulars, the Notice of the AGM along with the Annual Report 2024-25 is 
being sent only through e-mail, to those Members whose e-mail addresses are registered with the Company/ Registrar 
& Share Transfer Agent (RTA)/ Depository Participant/ Depositories. The Notice convening the 52 nd AGM has been 
uploaded on the website of the Company at www.mmpil.com and may also be accessed from the relevant section of the 
websites of the Stock Exchange i.e. National Stock Exchange of India Limited at www.nseindia.com.
8. Record Date, Book Closure and Dividend:
a) The Company has fixed Monday, 1st September, 2025 as the ‘Record Date’ for determining entitlement of 
members to dividend for the financial year ended 31st March, 2024, if approved at the AGM.
b) The Register of Members and the Share Transfer Books of the Company will be closed from Monday, 1st September, 
2025 to 8th September, 2025 (both days inclusive).
c) If the dividend, as recommended by the Board of Directors, is approved at the AGM, payment of such dividend 
subject to deduction of tax at source will be made within 30 days from the date of AGM as under:
i. To all Beneficial Owners in respect of shares held in dematerialised form as per the data as may be made 
available by the National Securities Depository Limited (“NSDL”) and the Central Depository Services 
(India) Limited (“CDSL”), collectively “Depositories”, as of end of day on Monday, 1st September, 2025;
9. Pursuant to Finance Act 2020, dividend income is taxable in the hands of shareholders w.e.f. 1st April, 2020 and the 
Company is required to deduct tax at source from dividend paid to shareholders at the prescribed rates. For the prescribed 
rates for various categories, please refer to the Finance Act, 2020 and the amendments thereof. The shareholders are 
requested to update their PAN with the Depository Participant (if shares held in electronic form) and Company / RTA (if 
shares held in physical form). A Resident individual shareholder with PAN and who is not liable to pay income tax can 
submit a yearly declaration in Form No. 15G / 15H, to avail the benefit of non-deduction of tax at source by e-mail to 
companysecretary@mmpil.com / tds@bigshareonline.com by 11:59 p.m. IST on 1st September, 2025. Shareholders are 
requested to note that in case their PAN is not registered, the tax will be deducted at a higher rate of 20%. Non-resident 
shareholders [including Foreign Institutional Investors (FIIs) / Foreign Portfolio Investors (FPIs)] can avail beneficial 
rates under tax treaty between India and their country of tax residence, subject to providing necessary documents i.e. 
No Permanent Establishment and Beneficial Ownership Declaration, Tax Residency Certificate, Form 10F, any other 
document which may be required to avail the tax treaty benefits. For this purpose the shareholder may submit the above 
documents (PDF / JPG Format) by e-mail to companysecretary@mmpil.com / tds@bigshareonline.com. The aforesaid 
declarations and documents need to be submitted by the shareholders by 11:59 p.m. IST on 1st September, 2025.
10. Members are also requested to intimate changes, if any, pertaining to their name, postal address, e-mail address, 
telephone/mobile numbers, PAN, registering of nomination, power of attorney registration, Bank Mandate details, etc. 
to Registrar/respective DPs as may be applicable.
11. Updation of bank mandate for receiving dividends directly in bank account through Electronic Clearing System or any 
other electronic means in a timely manner:
a. Shares held in electronic form: Members may please note that their bank details as furnished by the respective 
Depositories to the Company will be considered for remittance of dividend as per the applicable regulations of 
the Depositories and the Company will not entertain any direct request from such Members for change/addition/ 
deletion in such bank details. Accordingly, the Members holding shares in demat form are requested to update their 
Electronic Bank Mandate with their respective Depository Participants (DP).
12. Members seeking any information with regard to the financial statements or any matter to be placed at the AGM, are 
requested to write to the Company on or before 1st September, 2025 through e-mail on companysecretary@mmpil.com 
The same will be replied by the Company suitably.
13. To prevent fraudulent transactions, Members are advised to exercise due diligence and notify the Company of any 
change in address or demise of any Member as soon as possible. Members are also advised not to leave their demat 
account(s) dormant for long. Periodic statement of holdings should be obtained from the concerned DPs and holdings 
should be verified from time to time.

52th Annual Report 2024-25 MMP INDUSTRIES LIMITED

THE INTRUCTIONS OF SHAREHOLDERS FOR REMOTE E-VOTING ARE AS UNDER:
i. The voting period begins on Thursday, 4th September, 2025 at 9.00 a,m, and ends on Sunday, 7th September, 2025 at 5.00 
p.m. During this period shareholders’ of the Company, holding shares either in physical form or in dematerialized form, 
as on the cut-off date- 1st September, 2025 may cast their vote electronically. The e-voting module shall be disabled by 
Bigshare for voting thereafter.
ii. Shareholders who have already voted prior to the meeting date would not be entitled to vote at the meeting venue.
iii. Pursuant to SEBI Circular No. SEBI/HO/CFD/CMD/CIR/P/2020/242 dated 09.12.2020, under Regulation 44 of 
Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015, listed 
entities are required to provide remote e-voting facility to its shareholders, in respect of all shareholders’ resolutions. 
However, it has been observed that the participation by the public non-institutional shareholders/retail shareholders is at 
a negligible level.
 Currently, there are multiple e-voting service providers (ESPs) providing e-voting facility to listed entities in India. This 
necessitates registration on various ESPs and maintenance of multiple user IDs and passwords by the shareholders.
 In order to increase the efficiency of the voting process, pursuant to a public consultation, it has been decided to enable 
e-voting to all the demat account holders , by way of a single login credential, through their demat accounts/ 
websites of Depositories/ Depository Participants . Demat account holders would be able to cast their vote without 
having to register again with the ESPs, thereby, not only facilitating seamless authentication but also enhancing ease and 
convenience of participating in e-voting process.
iv. In terms of SEBI circular no. SEBI/HO/CFD/CMD/CIR/P/2020/242 dated December 9, 2020  on e-V oting facility 
provided by Listed Companies, Individual shareholders holding securities in demat mode are allowed to vote through 
their demat account maintained with Depositories and Depository Participants. Shareholders are advised to update their 
mobile number and email Id in their demat accounts in order to access e-V oting facility.
1. Pursuant to above said SEBI Circular , Login method for e-V oting and joining virtual meetings for Individual 
shareholders holding securities in Demat mode is given below:
Type of 
shareholders
 Login Method
Individual 
Shareholders 
holding 
securities in 
Demat mode 
with CDSL
1) Users who have opted for CDSL Easi / Easiest facility, can login through their existing user id and 
password. Option will be made available to reach e-V oting page without any further authentication. 
The URL for users to login to Easi / Easiest are https://web.cdslindia.com/myeasi/home/login or 
visit www.cdslindia.com and click on Login icon and select New System Myeasi.
2) After successful login the Easi / Easiest user will be able to see the e-V oting option for eligible 
companies where the evoting is in progress as per the information provided by company. On 
clicking the evoting option, the user will be able to see e-V oting page of BIGSHARE the e-V oting 
service provider and you will be re-directed to i-Vote website for casting your vote during the 
remote e-V oting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting. Additionally, there 
is also links provided to access the system of all e-V oting Service Providers i.e. BIGSHARE, so 
that the user can visit the e-V oting service providers’ website directly.
3) If the user is not registered for Easi/Easiest, option to register is available at https://web.cdslindia.
com/myeasi/Registration/EasiRegistration
4) Alternatively, the user can directly access e-V oting page by providing Demat Account Number 
and PAN No. from a link www.cdslindia.com home page. The system will authenticate the user by 
sending OTP on registered Mobile & Email as recorded in the Demat Account. After successful 
authentication, user will be able to see the e-V oting option where the evoting is in progress, and 
also able to directly access the system of all e-V oting Service Providers. Click on BIGSHARE and 
you will be re-directed to i-Vote website for casting your vote during the remote e-voting period.

with NSDL
1) If you are already registered for NSDL IDeAS facility, please visit the e-Services website of 
NSDL. Open web browser by typing the following URL: https://eservices.nsdl.com either on a 
Personal Computer or on a mobile. Once the home page of e-Services is launched, click on the 
“Beneficial Owner” icon under “Login” which is available under ‘IDeAS’ section. A new screen 
will open. You will have to enter your User ID and Password. After successful authentication, you 
will be able to see e-V oting services. Click on “Access to e-V oting” under e-V oting services and 
you will be able to see e-V oting page. Click on company name or e-V oting service provider name 
BIGSHARE and you will be re-directed to i-Vote website for casting your vote during the remote 
e-V oting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting.
2) If the user is not registered for IDeAS e-Services, option to register is available at https://eservices.
nsdl.com. Select “Register Online for IDeAS “Portal or click at https://eservices.nsdl.com/
SecureWeb/IdeasDirectReg.jsp
3) Visit the e-V oting website of NSDL. Open web browser by typing the following URL: https://
www.evoting.nsdl.com/ either on a Personal Computer or on a mobile. Once the home page of 
e-V oting system is launched, click on the icon “Login” which is available under ‘Shareholder/
Member’ section. A new screen will open. You will have to enter your User ID (i.e. your sixteen 
digit demat account number hold with NSDL), Password/OTP and a Verification Code as shown 
on the screen. After successful authentication, you will be redirected to NSDL Depository site 
wherein you can see e-V oting page. Click on company name or e-V oting service provider name 
BIGSHARE and you will be redirected to i-Vote website for casting your vote during the remote 
e-V oting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting

 Select event for which you are desire to vote under the dropdown option.
 Click on “VOTE NOW” option which is appearing on the right hand side top corner of the page.
 Cast your vote by selecting an appropriate option “IN FA VOUR”, “NOT IN FA VOUR” or “ABSTAIN” and click 
on “SUBMIT VOTE”. A confirmation box will be displayed. Click “OK” to confirm, else “CANCEL” to modify. 
Once you confirm, you will not be allowed to modify your vote.
 Once you confirm the vote you will receive confirmation message on display screen and also you will receive an 
email on your registered email id. During the voting period, members can login any number of times till they have 
voted on the resolution(s). Once vote on a resolution is casted, it cannot be changed subsequently.
 Shareholder can “ CHANGE PASSWORD” or “ VIEW/UPDATE PROFILE” under “ PROFILE” option on 
investor portal.

approved and appointed, M/s Khanuja Patra & Associates, Cost Accountants, Nagpur [Firm Registration No. 100247] as the 
Cost Auditors of the Company for the FY 2025-26 ending 31st March 2026 to audit the cost records of the Company at a

of pocket expenses, if any.
Pursuant to the provisions of Section 148(3) of the Companies Act, 2013 read with the Companies (Audit and Auditors) Rules, 
2014 (as amended), the remuneration as recommended by the Board of Directors, is subject to ratification by the Shareholders 
(Members) of the Company. None of the Director/s, Key Managerial Personnel (KMP) of the Company and their relatives are, 
in any way, concerned or interested, financially or otherwise, in the said Resolution.

(Members) in the interest of the Company.
Item No, 5
Appointment of Mr. Sachin Nirgudkar as Independent Director
On the recommendation of Nomination and Remuneration Committee and Board of Directors of the Company, has considered 
and approved, the appointment of Mr. Sachin Nirgudkar (DIN - 06890618), as an Additional Director (Category - Non-
executive, Independent) of the Company to hold the office till the conclusion of ensuing Annual General Meeting of the 
company.
In terms of Section 161(1) of the Companies Act, 2013 read with Articles of Association of the Company, Mr. Sachin Nirgudkar 
holds office as an Additional Director only up to the date of the forthcoming Annual General Meeting. The Company has 
received a notice from a member under Section 160 of the Companies Act, 2013, proposing the candidature of Mr. Sachin 
Nirgudkar for the office of Director (Category - Non-executive, Independent) of the Company, not liable to retire by rotation, 
for a term of 5 years i.e. from 8th August 2025 to 7th August, 2030.
The Company has received declaration from Mr. Sachin Nirgudkar to the effect that he meets the criteria of independence as 
provided in Section 149(6) of the Act read with the Rules framed thereunder and Regulation 16(1)(b) of the Securities and 
Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (“SEBI Listing Regulations”). 
In terms of Regulation 25(8) of SEBI Listing Regulations, he has confirmed that he is not aware of any circumstance or 
situation which exists or may be reasonably anticipated that could impair or impact his ability to discharge his duties. In the 
opinion of the Board, Sachin Nirgudkar fulfills the conditions specified in the Act, Rules and SEBI Listing Regulations for 
appointment as Independent Director and he is independent of the management of the Company.
The Board, based on the recommendation of Nomination and Remuneration Committee, considers that given his skills, 
integrity, expertise and experience, the association of Sachin Nirgudkar would be beneficial to the Company and it is desirable 
to avail his services as an Independent Director
Mr. Sachin Nirgudkar, may be deemed to be interested in the Resolution set out at Item No. 5 of the Notice with regard to his 
appointment as a Director (Category - Non-executive, Independent) of the Company.
Except Mr. Sachin Nirgudkar, for himself, being the appointee, none of the other Director/s, Key Managerial Personnel of the 
Company, and their relatives, are, in any way, concerned or interested, financially or otherwise, in the said Resolution.
The Board of Directors recommends the Special Resolution for approval of the Shareholders (Members) in the interest of the 
Company.

a) STATE OF COMPANY’S AFFAIRS

hereunder: -
 ALUMINIUM POWDERS
 The revenue for the segment in the FY 2024-25 was Rs. 43,835.43 Lakhs the revenue of Rs. 39,039.81 Lakhs in 
FY 2023-24.
 ALUMINIUM FOILS
 The revenue for the segment in the FY 2024-25 was Rs. 15,441.20 Lakhs over the revenue of Rs 10,703.89 Lakhs 
in FY 2023-24.
 ALUMINIUM CONDUCTORS & CABLES

of FY 2023-24.
[page 18]
52th Annual Report 2024-25 MMP INDUSTRIES LIMITED
13
 FUTURE PLANS / PROSPECTS:-
  Aluminium Powders
  The Aluminium Powders division delivered strong revenue growth of 25% year-on-year in Q4FY25 and 12% for 
the full year FY25, outperforming industry benchmarks and reflecting healthy demand.
  However, demand has been subdued in Q1FY26, particularly from the explosives segment. Export volumes have 
also declined due to the economic slowdown in Europe. Considering the current market conditions and the impact 
of the recent fire incident (note below), we anticipate single-digit growth for the Powder division in FY26.
  The Phase III capacity expansion of 2,500 MTPA (Pyro and Flake) has been partially commissioned and is expected 
to be fully operational by the end of Q2FY26.
  Aluminium Foils
  The Foil segment delivered exceptional revenue growth of 89% year-on-year in Q4FY25 and 44% for the full year 
FY25, driven by strong demand across all product categories and consistent supply of foil stock from Hindalco, 
our key supplier.
  Our brand, MMP, continues to gain strong traction among foil customers, reinforcing our position as a preferred 
vendor across diverse customer segments.
  Margins have improved, supported by robust demand and the recent withdrawal of export incentives by the Chinese 
Government, which has increased the cost of imports and benefited domestic producers.
  Aluminium Conductors and Cables
  The Aluminium Conductor and Cables division recorded strong revenue growth of 39% year-on-year in Q4FY25 
and 23% for the full year FY25, driven by sustained demand in the segment.
  We expect this positive momentum to continue in FY26 with further improved margins.
  MMP Electricals Private Limited [MEPL] – WOS
  During the period under review, the Company has incorporated its new WOS- Wholly Owned Subsidiary namely 
MMP Electricals Private Limited on 24th September, 2025, to the manufacturing of composite insulators for the 
power distribution and transmission sectors. This strategic decision marks the company’s diversification from 
aluminium products to electrical infrastructure products and exploit the market synergy with its conductor & cable 
division. This diversification is aimed to take advantage of the golden era of electrical infrastructural development 
in India.
  Commercial production is expected to commence in Q2FY26 of Phase I, we anticipate to reach full capacity by 
end of FY 25-26.
  Encouraged by strong market feedback and the promising growth potential, the company has already initiated 
Phase II expansion and will be completed by Q2 FY26-27.
b) CHANGE IN NATURE OF BUSINESS
 During the FY 2024-25 under review, the Board of Directors, though exploring addition to existing business and 
commercial activities, had neither been explored any change in nature of business and commercial activities for 
the Company nor there is a change in nature of business and commercial activities of the Company. As such, no 
specific details regarding change in nature of business activities are required to be given or provided.

11. CORPORATE SOCIAL RESPONSIBILITY (CSR)
 The Company believes in the Corporate Social Responsibility as an integral part of its business. Education specifically 
Education for Girls, Women, Poor, Under-privileged or Disabled (Divyang), Special Education, Research, Training & 
Skill Development, Environment, Health, Drinking Water, Rural Development, are some of the most critical problems 
that our country has been facing for years. One of the most effective direct and indirect solutions to solve these is an 
education, but a great number of peoples cannot afford to get them self-educated.
 Keeping this in mind, the Board of Directors through its CSR Committee has implemented certain CSR projects either 
directly or through implementing agency nominated by the CSR Committee of the Company. All the activities and 
programme/s covered under CSR are being monitored and implemented by the CSR Committee of the Company. The 
Company do confirm that the Company is in due compliance of the provisions of Section 135 of the Companies Act, 
2013 read with the Companies (Corporate Social Responsibility Policy) Rules, 2014. The CSR Project/s of the Company 
are in accordance with the provisions of Section 135 of, Schedule VII to, the Companies Act, 2013 read with the 
Companies (Corporate Social Responsibility Policy) Rules, 2014 and the Company’s CSR Policy. The Annual Report 
on the CSR Activities for the FY 2024-25 is given in the Annex - C, which forms part and parcel of the Board’s Report.

[page 24]
52th Annual Report 2024-25 MMP INDUSTRIES LIMITED
19
 The Board of Directors based on the recommendations of the Risk Management Committee of the Company, periodically 
reviews the Company’s risk assessment and minimization procedures to ensure that management identifies and controls 
risks through a properly defined framework. The details of the Risk Management Committee and its terms of reference 
are set out in the Corporate Governance Report forming part of this Report.
 Note on Fire Incident
 An explosion and fire incident occurred at the Aluminium Powder Plant in Umred on 11th April, 2025. The incident 
took place in the post-production area, not in the main production zone. It caused significant damage to the building and 
parts of the post-production machinery. Additionally, Aluminium Powder stock was destroyed in the finished goods (FG) 
godown and partially damaged in the work-in-progress (WIP) section
 Tragically, the incident resulted in 7 fatalities and 4 injuries. The company promptly announced and disbursed monetary 
compensation to the families of the deceased and the injured.
 Powder Plant operations (at Umred only) were suspended for over a month. However, all customer demands were 
fulfilled from the Bhandara and Hingna locations of the powder division. The company also had buffer inventory which 
has helped overcome this challenge.
 The estimated loss of ` 150 – 200 Mn, covering damage to plant and machinery, the building, and burnt stock, is fully 
insured. The company is actively working toward the early settlement of the insurance claim.

ANNUAL REPORT ON CORPORATE SOCIAL RESPONSIBILITY (CSR) ACTIVITIES
[Pursuant to Section 135 of the Companies Act, 2013 read with
the Companies (Corporate Social Responsibility Policy) Rules, 2014]
1. A brief outline on CSR Policy of the Company:
 The Board of Directors of the Company has constituted the Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) Committee to 
implement or monitor implementation of CSR activities as per the CSR Policy of the Company.
 The CSR activities are carried out and monitored mainly through in-house Departments of the Company. The 
Contributions or donations made to such other Organization or Institutions as may be permitted under the applicable 
provisions from time to time.
 The CSR Committee has formulated a comprehensive Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) Policy to cover various 
activities like Promotion of Education and Health Care, Rural Development, Water Conservation, Protection of Flora 
and Fauna, Environment Sustainability and other activities or project/s, which are in accordance with the provisions of 
Section 135 of, Schedule VII to, the Companies Act, 2013 read with the Companies (Corporate Social Responsibility 
Policy) Rules, 2014. The Members are requested to refer the CSR Policy of the Company for more specific details.

ANNEX – D TO BOARD’S REPORT
CONSERV ATION OF ENERGY, TECHNOLOGY ABSORPTION AND FOREIGN EXCHANGE EARNINGS / 
OUTGO
A. CONSERV ATION OF ENERGY
(i) The steps taken or impact on conservation of energy
 The Company has been giving utmost priority to conservation of various forms of energy used in the manufacturing 
process. The major conservation measures implemented so far are optimization of utility output to match process 
requirement thereby reducing wasteful running of equipment and timely replacement or servicing wherever 
required. Progressively, we are replacing normal Star Delta Starters with VFD’S for various machines.
 The major Energy conservation projects implemented in FY 2024-2025 are as follows:
1) Load Factor improvement above 85 % and its monitoring and control to ensure the incentives and government 
subsidy from MSEDCL implemented. Benefits accrued are sizable amounts.
2) During the FY 2024-25 the company has taken steps for replacing normal Star Delta Starters with VFD’S for 
various machines.
3) The Company is replacing progressively with IE3 Motors. This leads to conserve the energy to large extent.
4) The Company has replaced the old fluorescent lights with the new energy efficient LED lights.
 Total energy consumption and energy consumption per unit production is as follows: -
Sr. No. Particulars FY 2024-25 FY 2023-24
A Power & Fuel Consumption
1 Electricity
Purchased Units (in lakh) 286.976 216.77

(ii) The steps taken by the Company for utilizing alternate sources of energy
 As an environmentally conscious company and with a view to reduce the constantly increasing electricity costs, 
the company has through internal accruals, invested in 1.5 MW solar capacities at its two locations of Umred and 
Bhandara. These were operative and has lowered Electricity bills as expected. This will also reduce our carbon 
footprint and is first of the several steps which the company is taking towards a greener future.
(iii) The capital investment on energy conservation equipment’s
 The Company has made capital investment in 1.5 MW solar power at its two locations of Umred and Bhandara. 
In addition to that company has made investment in VFD’S and Energy efficient IEC motors for various machines 
for conservation of energy. Company is also investing in creating a solar park in coming financial year as approved 
by Board.
B. TECHNOLOGY ABSORPTION, RESEARCH AND DEVELOPMENT
(i) The efforts made towards technology absorption
 The Company has obtained know how from our four decade old sales and technology partner namely, A VL Metal 
Powders for production of various grades of aluminium powder for AAC applications.
(ii) The benefits derived like product improvement, cost reduction, product development or import substitution
 The Company has developed Aluminium Powder for AAC applications in India and for export market.
[page 35]
52th Annual Report 2024-25 MMP INDUSTRIES LIMITED
30
(iii) In case of imported technology (imported during the last three years reckoned from the beginning of the 
financial year)
 The Company has imported aluminum powder classifier equipment and technology with a view to offer wide range 
of classified aluminum powders to our customers.
(a) The details of the technology imported: Aluminum powder classifier equipment and technology to provide wide 
range of classified aluminum powder fractions.
(b) The year of import: 2019-20
(c) Whether the technology been fully absorbed: The technology is in an advanced stage of being getting absorbed.
(d) If not fully absorbed, areas where absorption has not taken place, and the reasons thereof: the technology is fully 
validated but our products are under evaluation for customer validation.
(iv) The expenditure incurred on Research and Development
 The Company does not have a separate independent research and development activity. As such, no material 
amount of expenditure was incurred on research and development activity of the Company.

We have conducted the Secretarial Audit of the compliance of applicable statutory provisions and the adherence to good 
corporate practices by MMP Industries Limited (hereinafter called as 'the Company'). Secretarial Audit was conducted in 
a manner that provided us a reasonable basis for evaluating the corporate conducts or statutory compliances and expressing 
our opinion thereon.
Based on our verification of the Company's books, papers, minute books, forms and returns filed, and other records maintained 
by the Company, the information, explanations and clarifications provided by the Company, its officers, agents and authorised 
representatives, during the conduct Of Secretarial Audit, review of mana gement representation letter alon g with Quarterly 
compliance reports by respective Department Head/s. Company Secretary. Chief Financial Officer, Chief Executive Officer, 
noted and taken on record by the Board of Directors of the Company, at their meeting/s, and verification of physical record/s of 
the Company. Some of which were obtained through electronic mode, we hereby report that, in Our opinion, the Company has, 
during the audit period coverin g the financial year 2024-2025 ended 31 March 2025 complied with the statutory provisions 
listed hereunder and also that the Company has proper Board-processes and compliance mechanism in place to the extent, in 
the manner and Subject to the reporting made hereinafter.

made available to us for the financial year 2024-2025 ended on 31st March 2025 according to the applicable provisions of:-

Our Secretarial Audit Report of even date is to be read along with this letter:-
1. Management's Responsibility
(a) It is the responsibility of the management of the Company to maintain secretarial records, devise proper systems 
to ensure compliance with the provisions of all applicable laws and regulations and to ensure that the systems are 
adequate and operate effectively.
(b) The compliance of the provisions of Corporate and other applicable laws, rules, regulations, standards is the 
responsibility of management. Our examination was limited to the verification of procedures on test basis.
2. Auditor's Responsibility
(a) Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these secretarial records, standards and procedures followed by the 
Company with respect to secretarial compliances.
(b) We have followed the audit practices and processes as were appropriate to obtain reasonable assurance about 
the correctness of the contents of the secretarial records. The verification was done on test basis to ensure that 
correct facts are reflected in secretarial records. We believe, the processes and practices that we followed provide a 
reasonable basis for our opinion. We also believe that audit evidence and information obtained from the Company's 
management is adequate and appropriate for us to provide a basis for our opinion.
(c) We have not verified the correctness and appropriateness of financial records and Books of Accounts of the 
Company.
(d) Wherever required. we have obtained the managements representation about the compliance of laws, rules and 
regulations and happening of events, etc.
3. Disclaimer
 The Secretarial Audit Report is neither an assurance as to the future viability of the Company nor of the efficacy or 
effectiveness with which the management has conducted the affairs of the Company. Further the Financial and Internal

MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS
ECONOMIC OVERVIEW AND OUTLOOK
Global Economy
As we progressed through FY 2024–25, the global economy continued its journey toward recovery, marked by resilience 
amidst persisting structural and geopolitical challenges. The post-pandemic landscape has settled into a phase of moderate and 
uneven growth, with inflation gradually converging toward central bank targets. Global headline inflation is estimated to have 
declined to around 5.9% in 2024, supported by easing commodity prices, improved labour market dynamics, and declining 
energy-related shocks.
The aftermath of the COVID-19 pandemic, compounded by disruptions caused by the Russia-Ukraine conflict and escalating 
tensions in the Middle East, has had a lasting impact on global supply chains, food and energy markets, and capital flows. 
These disruptions initially triggered inflationary pressures and forced central banks across the globe to implement synchronized 
monetary tightening.
Following a sharp slowdown in 2022 and a mild contraction in 2023, global output growth remained subdued during 2024, 
registering the third consecutive year of below-trend performance. This persistent deceleration reflects structural inefficiencies 
such as the misallocation of resources, declining private sector investment, a shrinking working-age population in key 
economies, and a general slowdown in total factor productivity. These trends have been further complicated by heightened 
geopolitical instability, especially in energy-sensitive regions.
According to the latest World Economic Outlook by the International Monetary Fund (IMF), advanced economies are showing 
signs of modest recovery, with GDP growth expected to inch up from 1.6% in 2023 to 1.7% in 2024 and 1.8% in 2025. For 
emerging markets and developing economies, growth is expected to remain steady at 4.2% in both 2024 and 2025. Low-
income developing countries are projected to see stronger momentum, with GDP growth rising from 4.0% in 2023 to 4.7% in 
2024 and 5.2% in 2025, as near-term challenges begin to fade.
Inflation expectations in advanced economies have declined meaningfully, and long-term inflation remains well-anchored. It 
is anticipated that many central banks will begin reducing policy interest rates in the second half of 2024, depending on the 
pace of disinflation and financial sector stability.
The moderation in inflation is underpinned by waning energy price shocks and an easing of supply-side constraints. Labour 
markets have also begun to rebalance, with job vacancies declining, a marginal uptick in unemployment, and enhanced labour 
supply driven in part by immigration.
Despite the steady macro indicators, the global economic outlook is tempered by several downside risks. These include possible 
commodity price spikes due to unresolved geopolitical conflicts, diverging disinflation paths leading to currency volatility, 
and financial sector vulnerabilities emerging from delayed impacts of high interest rates. Household debt burdens, particularly 
in economies with variable-rate mortgage exposures, are expected to weigh on consumption as refinancing pressures mount.
Public debt levels remain elevated, particularly in advanced and low-income economies where pandemic-related fiscal support 
led to a significant increase in debt-to-GDP ratios. Interest payments have consumed a growing share of fiscal revenues—
estimated at 14.3% for low-income countries in 2024—constraining fiscal space for growth-enhancing investments. 
Consequently, tighter fiscal consolidation strategies, including tax hikes and expenditure rationalization, are expected in many 
economies going forward.
On the trade front, while global merchandise trade growth has been sluggish, the outlook shows signs of improvement. 
According to the OECD’s February 2024 Interim Economic Outlook, a resurgence in semiconductor production and automobile 
sales, especially in Asia, is aiding recovery. The rebound in global air travel is also boosting services trade. However, logistical 
disruptions—particularly in the Red Sea region—have led to a rise in shipping costs and transit delays, affecting Asia-Europe 
trade routes.
As the global economy enters FY 2025–26, balancing inflation control with growth revival, mitigating geopolitical risks, and 
sustaining investor confidence will remain at the heart of economic policymaking worldwide.
Indian Economy
Amidst a challenging global backdrop, India continues to demonstrate robust economic momentum, solidifying its position as 
the world’s fastest-growing major economy. The country’s economic resilience in FY 2024–25 has been powered by strong 
domestic consumption, resilient rural demand, steady private investments, and sustained growth across core sectors such 
as manufacturing, construction, and services. India ends the fiscal year on a positive note—characterized by healthy GDP 
growth, moderating inflation, a stable external sector, and encouraging employment trends.
[page 41]
52th Annual Report 2024-25 MMP INDUSTRIES LIMITED
36
With a real GDP growth of 8.2% in FY 2023–24 and a projected growth of 7.2% for FY 2024–25, India remains on track to 
become the third-largest global economy by 2027, surpassing Japan and Germany. Additionally, it is expected to host the third-
largest stock market by 2030, driven by its expanding technological ecosystem, green energy transition, and demographic 
advantages.
Despite facing global headwinds such as rising crude oil prices, commodity market volatility, and persistent supply chain 
constraints, India’s macroeconomic fundamentals remain robust. The country's digital transformation, progressive policy 
reforms, and targeted sectoral incentives have created a conducive environment for long-term economic expansion.
Indian capital markets outperformed most emerging market peers during FY 2024, buoyed by strong foreign portfolio 
inflows—marking the highest cumulative FPI levels in the past five years. Equities remained the preferred investment class, 
with continued interest in government and corporate debt. This strong investor confidence reflects India's large consumer 
base, young and skilled labour force, and the government's reforms across education, digital infrastructure, logistics, and 
manufacturing.
Infrastructure development remained a central pillar of growth in FY 2024–25. Projects under the Bharatmala, Sagarmala, 
and Smart Cities Mission witnessed significant execution progress. These initiatives are enhancing regional connectivity, 
modernizing logistics, and reshaping the urban and industrial landscape. The government’s push for domestic semiconductor 
manufacturing and advanced technologies also aims to reduce import dependence and accelerate investment in AI and 
electronics manufacturing.
The industrial sector experienced a slower pace of growth in FY2024–25, with the Index of Industrial Production (IIP) 
expanding by 4.0%, marking a four-year low and the weakest post-Covid performance. In March 2025, IIP growth inched up 
to 3.0% year-on-year, slightly below expectations, but reflecting some recovery from February's 2.7% rise. The uptick was 
driven by improved performance in manufacturing (+3.0%) and electricity generation (+6.3%), although this was partially 
offset by subdued mining activity, which grew by just 0.4%. Among use-based segments, infrastructure and construction goods, 
consumer durables, and basic metals supported growth, while capital goods output moderated. On the downside, consumer 
non-durables witnessed a sharp contraction of 4.7%, continuing a two-month declining trend. The overall deceleration was 
influenced by weaker momentum in core sectors, base effects, and sectoral disparities, suggesting a cautious outlook for 
industrial recovery in the near term.
Provisional estimates for FY 2024–25 peg Real GDP growth at 6.5%, down from 9.2% in FY 2023–24, while Real GV A is 
expected to rise by 6.4% versus 8.6% earlier. Nominal GDP and GV A are projected to grow by 9.8% and 9.5%, respectively. 
Sectoral drivers in Q4 FY2024–25 included construction (+10.8%), public administration (+8.7%), and financial services 
(+7.8%). Inflation remained contained, with CPI inflation for FY2024–25 projected at 4.5%, continuing the disinflationary 
trend seen in late FY2023–24. India remains the world’s fastest-growing major economy, with real GDP growth estimated 
at 6.5% in 2024–25 and expected to continue at the same pace in 2025–26. This steady performance stands out amid global 
uncertainty.
Driven by strong domestic demand, easing inflation, robust capital markets, and rising exports, the economy shows resilience 
and balance. Indicators like record forex reserves, a manageable current account deficit, and rising foreign investment highlight 
growing global confidence in India’s long-term potential.
While the global environment poses risks—such as geopolitical instability, energy price shocks, and trade fragmentation—
India’s diversified economic structure and prudent policy framework position it well to manage volatility. Overall, real GDP 
growth for FY 2024–25 is forecasted at 7.2%, with quarterly projections ranging between 7.2% and 7.3%.
Industry Overview and Outlook
Aluminium Industry
Aluminium continues to be a cornerstone of modern industrial applications, valued for its unique combination of lightness, 
durability, corrosion resistance, and high conductivity. Its usage has become increasingly critical across multiple sectors—
ranging from aerospace and automotive to packaging, infrastructure, and electrical transmission. The growing demand for 
lighter, energy-efficient, and recyclable materials has further elevated aluminium’s strategic importance in the global transition 
towards sustainable industrial practices.
In the automotive sector, aluminium usage is steadily increasing as manufacturers aim to enhance fuel efficiency and meet 
stricter emission norms. Electric vehicle (EV) manufacturers, in particular, are adopting aluminium extensively to reduce 
vehicle weight and improve battery range, making it a vital enabler of green mobility. Simultaneously, aluminium’s presence 
in consumer electronics, construction materials, and packaging solutions underscores its indispensable role in both industrial 
and everyday applications.
[page 42]
52th Annual Report 2024-25 MMP INDUSTRIES LIMITED
37
The global aluminium market, valued at USD 243.89 billion in 2024, is projected to reach approximately USD 393.70 billion 
by 2032, growing at a CAGR of 6.2% (Fortune Business Insights, 2024). This expansion is driven by several structural factors, 
including the surge in demand for sustainable and lightweight materials in automotive, aerospace, and construction sectors 
(GlobeNewswire, 2025). Additionally, there is increasing adoption of recycled aluminium and advancements in manufacturing 
technologies such as precision alloying, automated smelting, and closed-loop recycling systems (GM Insights, 2025).
The automotive and beverage sectors continue to be major contributors to aluminium recycling, significantly promoting 
circular economy practices and reducing the industry's carbon footprint. With recycled aluminium consuming just 5% of 
the energy required for primary production, it is rapidly gaining regulatory and commercial preference (GM Insights, 2025). 
Moreover, rising consumer awareness and stringent environmental regulations are expected to further accelerate the transition 
toward low-emission, recyclable materials in the coming years (Reuters, 2025). The sector benefits from India’s ample bauxite 
reserves, with Odisha alone contributing more than half of the country’s production.
Key demand drivers include the transportation, construction, packaging, energy, aerospace, and defense sectors, increasingly 
propelled by government initiatives like Make in India, Production-Linked Incentive (PLI) schemes, and import substitution 
policies. For instance, aluminium’s use in lightweight freight rail wagons and electric vehicle components supports national 
efforts toward fuel efficiency and advanced manufacturing. The packaging industry—especially cans and foils—also continues 
to boost demand, driven by sustainability trends
Environmental compliance and sustainability initiatives are significant considerations, with producers like Vedanta committing 
to renewable power and green aluminium production rather than expanding coal-based capacity. Further, major capacity 
expansions, such as Vedanta’s planned multiple-million-tonne smelters in Odisha, reinforce both domestic production and 
downstream value creation
Moreover, India’s packaging industry—especially in the food and beverage segment—is experiencing a marked rise in 
aluminium consumption, driven by evolving consumer preferences for convenience, hygiene, and sustainability. Aluminium 
foil and rigid containers are preferred choices due to their superior barrier properties, reusability, and compliance with safety 
norms.
Despite these positive developments, the industry faces operational and regulatory challenges. Evolving environmental norms, 
energy efficiency mandates, and emission control requirements have increased compliance costs. Aluminium manufacturing 
is energy-intensive, and aligning with green production standards demands significant capital investment in technology 
upgradation, waste management systems, and clean energy sourcing. While these regulations are essential for long-term 
environmental sustainability, they pose short-term pressures on profitability and require industry players to recalibrate their 
operations.
Looking ahead, the aluminium industry in FY 2026 is expected to benefit from structural shifts in global supply chains, 
increasing integration of green technologies, and policy incentives for sustainable manufacturing. Companies with strong 
backward integration, efficient recycling systems, and technological capabilities are well-positioned to capitalize on the 
emerging opportunities while navigating the evolving regulatory landscape.
INDUSTRY OR SECTOR WISE PERFORMANCE
Aluminium Powder
Aluminium powder, a fine, silvery-white, lightweight material produced through the milling of aluminium metal, plays a 
critical role across a wide array of industrial applications. Owing to its high reactivity, thermal conductivity, and corrosion 
resistance, it is widely used in industries such as aerospace, automotive, construction, defence, and energy. Its flammable 
nature and high surface area make it especially suitable for applications requiring combustion or energy release, such as 
pyrotechnics and explosives.
In recent years, aluminium powder has become increasingly vital in modern manufacturing technologies, particularly additive 
manufacturing and 3D printing, where its strength-to-weight ratio and ability to withstand high temperatures enhance the 
quality of complex components. The automotive industry continues to drive demand, leveraging aluminium powder to develop 
lightweight, fuel-efficient, and low-emission vehicles. Simultaneously, the aerospace sector, fuelled by advancements in 
aviation and defence, is integrating aluminium powder in the development of precision-engineered, lightweight components.
Another significant area of growth is renewable energy, where aluminium powder is used in the fabrication of solar panels, 
wind turbine components, and energy-efficient conductors. Its recyclability further aligns with global efforts toward sustainable 
development and circular economy practices.
[page 43]
52th Annual Report 2024-25 MMP INDUSTRIES LIMITED
38
The moderate but steady growth is fueled by increasing urbanization, rapid development in the construction and automotive 
sectors, technological innovation, and rising consumption in paints, coatings, and pigments. The Asia-Pacific region, particularly 
India and China, remains the largest contributor to demand, driven by aggressive industrialization and infrastructure expansion.
In the Indian context, the aluminium powder market reached 43,977 tons in 2023 and is expected to grow to 71,142 tons by 
2032, reflecting a strong CAGR of 5.49%. This significant growth is underpinned by rising applications across sectors such as 
explosives, chemical manufacturing, paint production, and metallurgy. Additionally, the increasing utilization of aluminium 
powder in automotive coatings, thermal spray technologies, and additive manufacturing is contributing to sustained market 
momentum within the country.
Construction and Housing
India’s construction sector continues to be a cornerstone of the country’s economic development, with infrastructure and 
housing forming the foundation of national growth. Recognized as a high-priority area by the Government of India, the sector 
has seen unprecedented levels of investment, policy support, and execution momentum in FY 2024–25.
The Union Budget 2024-25 builds upon the transformative step taken in 2023, maintaining robust fiscal support for 
infrastructure development. This year’s capital expenditure allocation stands at `11.11 lakh crore (approximately 3.4% of 
GDP), aimed at fostering modern, connected, and sustainable urban ecosystems.. Alongside fiscal support, regulatory reforms 
like GST rationalization and RERA implementation have increased transparency, strengthened investor confidence, and 
revived momentum in the real estate segment.
India has emerged as the third-largest recipient of Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) in the construction and real estate space, 
reflecting strong investor confidence. This trend is further reinforced by rising private equity and sovereign fund participation, 
transforming the sector into a technology-driven, globally competitive landscape.
The construction sector’s contribution to GDP is at historic highs, with Gross Value Added (GV A) from construction growing 
by 9% in FY 2023–24. Growth continues to be propelled by ambitious public infrastructure investments across transportation, 
urban housing, water resource management, and renewable energy sectors.
Several flagship government initiatives are driving this momentum:
 Bharatmala Pariyojana: Development of 34,800 km of national highways
 Sagarmala Programme: Over 800 port and logistics infrastructure projects
  PM Gati Shakti: Integrated infrastructure planning and execution
 Greenfield Airports: 21 new sites under development, including Navi Mumbai and Jewar International Airport
These strategic initiatives have not only enhanced India’s infrastructure backbone but have also unlocked long-term 
opportunities for contractors, material suppliers, and allied industries.
This infrastructure surge directly benefits aluminium-intensive applications, boosting demand for foils, cables, structural 
components, and aluminium powder used in concrete and coating formulations. Additionally, the expansion of tunneling and 
foundation work has increased the requirement for industrial explosives, further linking the construction boom with upstream 
material industries.
Explosives for Mining and Infrastructure
Aluminium powder plays a pivotal role in the explosives industry, where it enhances the energy output and thermal performance 
of explosives used in mining, construction, defence, and infrastructure. Its rapid oxidation reaction makes it essential in 
producing thermite mixtures, slurry explosives, and ANFO (Ammonium Nitrate Fuel Oil). It also finds application in rocket 
propellants and pyrotechnics, where it provides brilliance and combustion strength.
The global explosives market, valued at USD 53.2 billion in 2024, is expected to reach USD 67.87 billion by 2028, growing 
at a CAGR of 6.3%. The rising demand is fueled by:
 Intensified construction and mining activity,
 Government investments in infrastructure and defence,
 Rapid urbanization and increased tunnel development.
Countries such as India, China, UAE, and Saudi Arabia have led large-scale tunnel and highway construction using industrial 
explosives. India's infrastructure growth, combined with its strategic mining and mineral exploration agenda, has made it a 
significant consumer of industrial explosives.
[page 44]
52th Annual Report 2024-25 MMP INDUSTRIES LIMITED
39
India is also undergoing regulatory transformation in this space. The Explosives Bill, 2024—intended to replace the colonial-
era Explosives Act of 1884—proposes a modern framework for licensing, usage limits, compliance, and penalties. Once 
enacted, it is expected to create a safer, more accountable environment for handling high-energy materials.
While growth prospects are strong, the industry must navigate operational risks related to environmental safety, logistics, 
and regulatory oversight. Manufacturers are increasingly required to adopt international best practices, including real-time 
inventory tracking, GPS-based transport monitoring, and controlled storage systems.
With aluminium powder at the core of energy-intensive formulations, MMP Industries Limited remains well-positioned to 
serve this growing market with quality-assured, application-specific powder grades.
Aluminium Foil
The aluminium foil industry continues to experience robust growth in FY 2024–25, driven by evolving consumer preferences, 
rapid urbanisation, and increased demand for convenience-based packaging. The shift towards on-the-go consumption, rising 
penetration of quick-service restaurants, and the proliferation of e-commerce in food delivery have significantly accelerated 
the adoption of flexible, lightweight, and hygienic packaging materials. In this context, aluminium foil has emerged as a key 
packaging solution due to its superior barrier properties, thermal stability, and recyclability.
Widely used across food, beverage, and pharmaceutical industries, aluminium foil provides effective protection against 
moisture, light, oxygen, and microbial contamination, thereby extending product shelf life and preserving quality. In 
retail environments such as supermarkets, bakeries, and food stalls, aluminium-based pouches and wraps ensure hygiene, 
convenience, and compliance with safety standards.
The demand surge in ready-to-eat meals and packaged foods, especially among India’s growing working population, has 
significantly expanded the market for aluminium foil in the FMCG segment. At the same time, the proliferation of electric 
vehicles (EVs) and mobile technologies has increased the requirement for lithium-ion batteries, where aluminium foil serves 
as a critical component for cell sealing and current collection.
The sustainability profile of aluminium continues to give it a competitive edge. According to industry estimates, approximately 
75% of all aluminium ever produced remains in circulation—a testament to its recyclability and environmental efficiency. 
Recycling aluminium requires significantly less energy than primary production, thereby reducing greenhouse gas emissions 
and aligning with ESG goals of both manufacturers and consumers. This sustainability advantage is prompting brands to 
replace plastic and multi-layered composites with recyclable aluminium alternatives.
Beyond the food and pharmaceutical sectors, aluminium foil is also gaining traction in electronics, insulation, and construction 
applications. Its light weight, corrosion resistance, and insulating capabilities make it suitable for thermal shielding, power 
transmission equipment, and precision electronic components. The pharmaceutical industry continues to rely on aluminium 
foil for strip packing, blister packs, and sachets, given its non-toxic and tamper-evident characteristics.
The growth is being driven by robust demand from packaging innovations, energy storage technologies, and the healthcare 
sector. However, the market's upward trajectory may face headwinds due to geopolitical trade interventions, such as anti-
dumping duties imposed by the U.S. and other jurisdictions on aluminium products originating from countries like China and 
Oman. These trade measures may disrupt global pricing structures and sourcing strategies, especially for foil manufacturers 
that depend heavily on export markets.In the Indian context, the aluminium foil segment is well-positioned for sustained 
expansion, supported by:
 the government’s focus on sustainable and recyclable packaging,
 growing urban consumption,
 deeper penetration of organized retail, and
 rapid improvements in cold-chain infrastructure.
Enterprises with backward integration, recycling capabilities, and diversified product portfolios are expected to benefit most 
from these evolving market dynamics.
A notable development in FY 2024–25 is the implementation of anti-dumping duties on aluminium foil (up to 80 microns 
thickness) imported from China, effective March 2025 for a five-year period. This trade safeguard, aimed at curbing the 
adverse effects of underpriced imports, is expected to:
 stabilize domestic prices,
 improve capacity utilization for Indian manufacturers, and
 stimulate long-term investments in quality enhancement and technological innovation.
[page 45]
52th Annual Report 2024-25 MMP INDUSTRIES LIMITED
40
Overall, this move aligns with broader efforts to fortify the domestic aluminium value chain and build a more resilient and 
globally competitive manufacturing ecosystem.
Aluminium Conductors and Cables
In FY 2024–25, aluminium conductors continue to serve as a cornerstone in the global power transmission and distribution 
infrastructure. Known for their high tensile strength, corrosion resistance, and cost-effectiveness, aluminium conductors are 
extensively used in the construction of overhead power lines and grid networks, particularly across emerging and developing 
economies. With the ongoing transition to sustainable energy and expanding electrification initiatives, the demand for reliable 
and efficient transmission infrastructure has surged, placing aluminium at the forefront of energy sector materials.
While copper offers higher conductivity, aluminium’s lower density allows for greater conductivity per unit weight, making 
it a lighter and more economical alternative. Modern conductor designs often involve aluminium strands wound around 
composite or reinforced cores to optimize strength, reduce sag, and extend transmission range. Over time, older steel-reinforced 
conductors have shown susceptibility to corrosion, prompting a wave of replacement activity across ageing grids—further 
stimulating demand for advanced aluminium-based solutions.
. The steady growth is driven by the continued rollout of electrification initiatives, expansion of renewable energy infrastructure, 
modernization of smart grids, and increased investment in high-voltage transmission systems. Aluminium conductors are 
increasingly preferred for long-distance power transmission owing to their light weight, lower sag, and cost efficiency—
qualities that make them particularly suitable for improving electricity access across remote and challenging geographies.
In India, the momentum remains strong as the country retains its position as the third-largest producer and consumer of 
electricity globally. As of April 2024, India’s installed power capacity stood at 441.97 GW, with an ambitious goal of 
achieving 500 GW of renewable energy capacity by 2030. This is being supported by a range of strategic infrastructure 
projects, including:
 The Raipur–Visakhapatnam Expressway (`20,000 crore),
 The Kala Amb smart grid initiative (`19.44 crore),
 And `19,518 crore allocated for metro rail development under the Union Budget 2023–24.
These investments reflect India’s broader shift toward infrastructure-led economic growth, clean energy integration, and 
technologically advanced grid systems.
The domestic cables and conductors industry is evolving to meet these demands through innovations in thermal performance, 
corrosion resistance, and eco-friendly design. While the sector is benefitting from long-term infrastructure trends, it continues 
to face challenges such as global economic uncertainty, supply chain disruptions, and volatile raw material prices. In 
response, industry players are increasingly adopting strategies such as localized sourcing, recycling infrastructure, and green 
manufacturing practices to align with ESG commitments and reduce reliance on imports.
Pesticides and Aluminium in Agriculture
The global agriculture sector faces rising pressure to increase productivity while managing the risks posed by pests, climate 
variability, and shrinking arable land. In this context, pesticides play a vital role in safeguarding crops against insects, 
weeds, and diseases, thereby ensuring food security and yield optimization. Their significance has grown in recent years as 
governments aim to enhance agricultural output and reduce post-harvest losses.
As of FY 2025–26, Asia remains the dominant consumer of pesticides, accounting for over half of global usage. India continues 
to rank 12th globally and 3rd in Asia, following China and Turkey. According to Allied Market Research, the global pesticide 
market, valued at USD 45.7 billion in 2022, is projected to reach USD 92.6 billion by 2032, registering a CAGR of 7.5%. 
The Indian pesticide market, estimated at USD 321.5 million in 2024, is forecast to grow at a CAGR of 8.01% through 2030, 
driven by India’s expansive agricultural footprint, proactive government subsidies, and a growing shift toward scientific and 
precision farming practices.
While pesticides remain indispensable for crop protection, their overuse or misapplication continues to raise serious 
environmental and public health concerns, underscoring the need for integrated pest management (IPM) and precision 
agriculture.
Amid this agricultural evolution, aluminium is emerging as a key enabler of sustainable farming. Traditionally associated with 
industries like construction and aerospace, aluminium is now finding innovative applications in agriculture due to its light 
weight, durability, non-toxicity, and resistance to corrosion. Its agricultural contributions span a range of use cases:
[page 46]
52th Annual Report 2024-25 MMP INDUSTRIES LIMITED
41
 Aluminium compounds, such as aluminium sulphate, are used as adjuvants to boost pesticide performance, adjust pH 
levels in formulations, and act as flocculants in irrigation water treatment.
 Aluminium salts are incorporated in fungicides for their effective antifungal action.
 Aluminium foils and barrier packaging are critical for safe storage and transportation of agrochemicals, minimizing 
degradation and extending shelf life.
 In precision farming, aluminium is increasingly used in lightweight spraying devices, sensor casings, and agricultural 
drones, improving the accuracy of pesticide application and reducing environmental impact.
 For greenhouses and polyhouses, aluminium frameworks offer structural stability, weather resistance, and long-term 
sustainability in chemically active environments.
As India advances toward mechanized, resource-efficient, and climate-resilient agriculture, aluminium’s role is set to expand. 
Its integration across crop protection, irrigation, and equipment manufacturing reflects a broader trend of technological 
convergence aimed at meeting the twin goals of agricultural productivity and environmental stewardship.
COMPANY OVERVIEW
MMP Industries Limited (“MMP” or “the Company”) is engaged in the production of a diverse range of aluminium-based 
products, operating from its manufacturing facilities located in and around Nagpur, Maharashtra. The Company’s product 
portfolio includes pyro and flake aluminium powders, atomised aluminium powders, aluminium foils, aluminium pastes, and 
aluminium conductors and cables.
The Company’s aluminium powders—comprising pyro, flake, and atomised variants—cater to multiple industrial segments, 
including:
  Construction, for the manufacture of AAC (Autoclaved Aerated Concrete) blocks;
 Mining and infrastructure, in the form of aluminised slurry explosives;
 Agriculture, for use in pesticide formulations;
 Defence, for applications in ammunition;
 Railways, in thermite welding processes;
 Fireworks, for pyrotechnic products.
In addition, aluminium foils produced by MMP are widely used in the pharmaceutical and food packaging sectors due to their 
excellent barrier properties and hygienic characteristics. The Company’s aluminium conductors and cables serve the power 
transmission sector, playing a critical role in the installation of overhead transmission lines across the country.
Upcoming Projects
To augment its product portfolio and support the growing demand in power infrastructure and grid modernization, the 
Company is actively expanding its operations through strategic investments in its wholly owned subsidiaries:
 MMP Electricals Private Limited is in the process of setting up a new manufacturing facility for composite insulators, 
which are widely used in high-voltage transmission and distribution networks. These insulators offer high mechanical 
strength, improved resistance to environmental degradation, and longer service life compared to conventional ceramic or 
glass insulators. The addition of composite insulators will enable the Company to offer a comprehensive range of power 
transmission products to utilities and infrastructure developers.
 MMP Cables Private Limited is developing a manufacturing facility for Low V oltage Power Cables (LVPC), marking 
the Company’s entry into the fast-growing segment of energy-efficient and safety-compliant cable solutions. LVPCs 
are essential for domestic, commercial, and industrial electrical systems, and the new facility will cater to increasing 
demand driven by urban electrification, smart buildings, and renewable energy installations.
These initiatives align with the Company’s long-term strategy of strengthening backward and forward integration across the 
aluminium value chain while diversifying into high-growth, high-demand segments within the electrical and infrastructure 
sectors.
The company plans to establish a wire rod manufacturing facility as part of a backward integration initiative, with an 
investment of ` 130 - 150 Mn,. This project will be undertaken under a separate wholly owned subsidiary—MMP Alutech 
Private Limited. The initiative is aimed at improving margins for the Cable/Conductor and LVPC segments while reducing 
reliance on external wire rod procurement and ensuring a stable supply chain.

ASSOCIATE COMPANIES / JOINT VENTURE COMPANIES
The Company has a joint venture with Toyo Aluminium K.K. of Japan, Toyal MMP India Private Limited (TMI), for the 
manufacturing of special grades of aluminium paste. The Company holds 26% equity in the new joint venture.
Star Circlips & Engineering Limited is an associate Company engaged in the manufacture of circlips, retaining rings, washers, 
shims and formed components mainly used in auto and auto component industries in which the Company holds 26.06% equity.
EXPORT
MMP primarily exports its products to three main regions in the world that include Europe, Africa, and the Middle East. The 
Company’s products have started being sold in Japan through our JV company TMI.
OPPORTUNITIES
Demand for aluminium is expected to pick up as the scenario improves for user industries like power, infrastructure, coal, 
mining, and transportation.
The Company will also have access to various global Toyo marketing channels, which will help both the Aluminium powder 
and paste business. Since the Company produces certain powders not in the range of our JV partner Toyo Aluminium KK, this 
is an opportunity to open business potential in Japan through TMI.
The company has entered into a long term sale arrangement for special grades of concrete powders for AAC Industry with 
its long term partner in Europe. This will be a volume business and as the economic situation stabilises in Europe, we expect 
improved margins in the coming years from this new long term opportunity.
With the installation of special machinery and process development assistance from our JV partner, the Company will produce 
special fractions of powders for bulk usage in space applications and specialized defence applications.
In parallel, the imposition of anti-dumping duties on aluminium foil (up to 80 microns) imported from China, effective March 
2025 for a period of five years, marks a key policy shift aimed at addressing trade imbalances. This decision is expected to 
ensure pricing stability, improve domestic capacity utilization, and encourage sustained investment in technology and quality, 
thereby enhancing the competitiveness of the broader aluminium ecosystem.
THREATS
Economic and industrial growth is going to be adversely impacted for the foreseeable future due to external challenges. The 
uncertainty of how long this will persist cannot be predicted.
The volatility in the prices of important raw material namely Aluminium is another challenge with the rapidly changing global 
economic scenario. This impacts business of all aluminium user segments.
The manufacturing and handling of Aluminium Powder is inherently hazardous and there is always a risk of accidents in the 
plant. MMP is OHSAS and EMS certified and gives prime importance to the safety of its people. The Company conducts 
regular safety audits and trainings to mitigate risks of an accident.
